What a run is. One invocation of the framework on one design with one mode: here conv_layer with mac_18x19_fixed_pt_mode.

What happened. 2 rewrites measured (Pass 1, Pass 2); kept: Pass 1 (the lowest area x delay, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 5.81%). Loop stopped by: no gain beyond noise (gain -2.02% against first, min-gain 0.0581 (baseline adw_reg_seed_spread_frac)).

Result. Area x delay -28.37% against the unmodified design on the same layout (koios_small, 140 x 140): 2.77699e+08 vs 3.87672e+08 (baseline: median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them)); verification: not recorded in the run record.

Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
- output ports: out[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified conv_layer under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.57582 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 84 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 6.1312e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 294638, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.00726e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in qmult (the multiply's module; the registers' modules not read): one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), from a register straight into the multiply, then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.56 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in qmult (another multiply, $mul~690; the registers' modules not read), is the same shape as the slowest stage, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns into the multiply, 1.45 from the multiply into the LUT, 5.496 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 4.5
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (i.e. the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2777, zero-extended 16->18); .b({3'b0, b_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2778, zero-extended 16->19); .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] carries the running sum that the baseline held in add_out_reg. The block's operand registers replace mul_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781) and the block's accumulator replaces add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2806); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances and both of those registers are deleted.

PLAN:

Candidates. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (12 live PEs x 7 matmul slices; conv_layer.v:2570-2588, 1309-1585). All 84 are converted; none is left.

What the region is. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped register the operands; qmult forms the unsigned 16x16 product into 32 bits (conv_layer.v:2854 — both operands are plain unsigned vectors, so this is an unsigned product and matches the block's unsigned ports and zero-extension exactly, and 16x16 never exceeds 32 of the block's 37 bits); mul_out_reg registers it; qadd adds it to add_out_reg; add_out_reg registers the sum; a saturating down-cast and the out register follow. The accumulator recurrence add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is exactly what the block computes, except that add_in1/add_in2 (conv_layer.v:2807-2808) overwrite the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped whenever eltwise_mode & eltwise_add. The block has no load path and no enable, so that overwrite cannot go inside it, and the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add/reset independently (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:88-89, drive_conv_seq_mac.vh:5-11 — the verdict build forces no reset at a phase boundary), so no assumption about phase boundaries may be used. Hence T2: the accumulation goes into the block and the overwrite is reproduced outside it as a registered offset.

The rewrite (per seq_mac). Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their reset exactly as they are; they are read by the eltwise path, so they stay outside the block. Delete mult_u1, mul_out_reg, add_u1 and add_out_reg. Add:

wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a_flopped}), .b({3'b0, b_flopped}), .out(blk_out));

wire        elt_sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;
wire [16:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

reg  [16:0] elt_reg;    // the eltwise-add value, 17 bits is its full width
reg         sel_reg;    // 1 in the cycle whose accumulator value came from the eltwise path
reg  [31:0] corr;       // offset: accumulator value minus the block's running sum

always @(posedge clk) begin
  elt_reg <= elt_sum;                      // no reset: read only when sel_reg is 1, by when it is defined
  if (reset) begin
    sel_reg <= 1'b0;
    corr    <= 32'b0;
  end else begin
    sel_reg <= elt_sel;
    if (sel_reg) corr <= {15'b0, elt_reg} - blk_out[31:0];
  end
end

wire [31:0] acc_val = sel_reg ? {15'b0, elt_reg} : (blk_out[31:0] + corr);

Then leave the down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) textually unchanged except that every add_out_reg in them becomes acc_val, with the same bit indices.

Why it matches on every cycle. Write X[k] for a register's value in cycle k and g[k] for a combinational signal during cycle k; r is reset, E is elt_sel, A[k] = a_flopped[k]+b_flopped[k]. The original: s[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (E[k] ? A[k] : s[k]+m[k]) with m[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : a_flopped[k]*b_flopped[k]. The block, driven by a_flopped/b_flopped and by the same reset, has operand registers p[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (a_flopped[k], b_flopped[k]), so its product term p_a[k]*p_b[k] equals m[k] cycle for cycle (0*0 = 0 under reset), and out[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : out[k] + m[k] — the original recurrence with the eltwise branch removed. The invariant maintained is: sel_reg[k] implies s[k] = elt_reg[k], and otherwise s[k] = out[k] + corr[k] mod 2^32. It holds after any reset (all of sel_reg, corr, out, s are 0); it is re-established on an eltwise cycle (sel_reg[k+1]=1, elt_reg[k+1]=A[k]=s[k+1]); and on a non-eltwise cycle corr[k+1] is elt_reg[k]-out[k] = s[k]-out[k] when sel_reg[k] and is held otherwise, while both out and s take the same +m[k], so out[k+1]+corr[k+1] = s[k+1]. Only the low 32 bits of the block's modulo-2^37 sum are used, and the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of its addends, so the block's wider wrap is invisible. Latency, reset behaviour and out are therefore bit-identical on every cycle, including back-to-back eltwise cycles, eltwise->MAC transitions with no reset, and eltwise_mode & ~eltwise_add (where E=0 and the plain MAC path is used). No added register is relied on before the first reset: corr and sel_reg are reset-cleared, and elt_reg is read only when sel_reg is 1, which cannot happen before a reset-released eltwise cycle.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, replacing the 84 soft multiplies that synthesis had already put on 84 DSP tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode, one per tile. The mode holds two blocks per tile and all 12 blocks of a matmul slice see the identical reset expression (effective_rst | (eltwise_mode & ~ready_for_eltwise_op), conv_layer.v:2528/2001/2647) and the one clock, so they may legally pair into 6 tiles per slice (42 total); the baseline packer paired none of the equivalent pairs, so the count reported is the conservative 84 — the same tile count as the baseline, i.e. the rewrite is not expected to pay or save tile area, and its gain must come from delay and from the soft logic it moves.

Stages and the critical path. No two blocks are ever in series: the longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, 2.219 ns inside the block (input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup), well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The stages the rewrite leaves, per instance:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> block input register: 0.060 + 1.627 (register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> 17-bit elt_sum -> elt_reg: about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + 0.019 = 2.2 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> corr: 0.060 + 0.96 (block into soft logic) + 1.14 (one soft adder level) + 0.019 = 2.18 ns.
  • slowest: block out -> 32-bit add blk_out+corr -> sel_reg mux -> down-cast -> out flip-flop: 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + about two further soft levels (the 32-bit mux folds into the down-cast's per-bit LUT, whose inputs become sel/elt_reg bit/sign/overflow/sum bit — five, one LUT6; the 16-bit OR that forms the overflow flag is the other level) + 0.019, about 4.5 ns.

This is the one stage the rewrite lengthens: in the baseline the 32-bit add ended at add_out_reg and the down-cast was a separate short stage; here the add and the down-cast share a stage. It is deliberate — it is the price of putting the accumulator register inside the block — and at about 4.5 ns it still sits below the baseline's 5.57582 ns and below the 5.496 ns second-slowest stage. Both baseline stages that set those numbers are qmult multiply stages (register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop) in two different seq_mac instances; all 84 such stages are removed by this rewrite, since all 84 instances are converted, so the new worst stage is the one above.

Soft logic moved. Out of the design: mul_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), add_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), the two 32-bit add_in1/add_in2 muxes (about 64 LUTs) and the 32-bit qadd — these leave the baseline's slowest stage (the multiply stage, which disappears entirely) and the accumulate stage. Into the design: elt_reg (17 flip-flops), sel_reg (1), corr (32 flip-flops plus a 32-bit load mux), a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit adder. Net per instance is roughly -14 flip-flops, -94 LUTs and +49 carry-chain bits, so used logic-block area should land near the baseline's, with the gain coming from the roughly 20% shorter critical path; wirelength should move little, the block's 32 output bits now feeding two adders instead of one register.

Register stages, before and after. Before: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> product -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> sum -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4); the product is registered 1 stage before the sum. After: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> block operand registers (stage 2, where the product is formed) -> block accumulator out (stage 3) -> +corr/mux/down-cast -> out (stage 4). Identical count and identical latency; no register is added to or removed from the operand-to-result path, and the three added registers (elt_reg, sel_reg, corr) sit beside it, not in it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's eltwise-add result were written to its own register instead of overwriting the MAC accumulator (conv_layer.v:2807-2808), or if reset were guaranteed asserted at every eltwise-to-MAC boundary — which the conv_layer FSM in fact does, since start_mat_mul drops and clk_cnt returns to 0 while op still reads 2'b10, holding mac_reset high (conv_layer.v:1143-1155, 2001, 2647) — then the block's out could be used as add_out_reg directly, deleting corr, the 32-bit subtractor and the 32-bit output adder and taking about 1.1 ns off the stage above. The standalone seq_mac testbench does not honour that boundary, so the rewrite above cannot rely on it.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Call 2 · plan reviewer · APPROVE · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 16 · out 11797 · reasoning 9504 · cache read 182993 · cache write 34015 · 161.0 s
prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 4.5
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (i.e. the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2777, zero-extended 16->18); .b({3'b0, b_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2778, zero-extended 16->19); .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] carries the running sum that the baseline held in add_out_reg. The block's operand registers replace mul_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781) and the block's accumulator replaces add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2806); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances and both of those registers are deleted.

PLAN:

Candidates. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (12 live PEs x 7 matmul slices; conv_layer.v:2570-2588, 1309-1585). All 84 are converted; none is left.

What the region is. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped register the operands; qmult forms the unsigned 16x16 product into 32 bits (conv_layer.v:2854 — both operands are plain unsigned vectors, so this is an unsigned product and matches the block's unsigned ports and zero-extension exactly, and 16x16 never exceeds 32 of the block's 37 bits); mul_out_reg registers it; qadd adds it to add_out_reg; add_out_reg registers the sum; a saturating down-cast and the out register follow. The accumulator recurrence add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is exactly what the block computes, except that add_in1/add_in2 (conv_layer.v:2807-2808) overwrite the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped whenever eltwise_mode & eltwise_add. The block has no load path and no enable, so that overwrite cannot go inside it, and the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add/reset independently (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:88-89, drive_conv_seq_mac.vh:5-11 — the verdict build forces no reset at a phase boundary), so no assumption about phase boundaries may be used. Hence T2: the accumulation goes into the block and the overwrite is reproduced outside it as a registered offset.

The rewrite (per seq_mac). Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their reset exactly as they are; they are read by the eltwise path, so they stay outside the block. Delete mult_u1, mul_out_reg, add_u1 and add_out_reg. Add:

wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a_flopped}), .b({3'b0, b_flopped}), .out(blk_out));

wire        elt_sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;
wire [16:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

reg  [16:0] elt_reg;    // the eltwise-add value, 17 bits is its full width
reg         sel_reg;    // 1 in the cycle whose accumulator value came from the eltwise path
reg  [31:0] corr;       // offset: accumulator value minus the block's running sum

always @(posedge clk) begin
  elt_reg <= elt_sum;                      // no reset: read only when sel_reg is 1, by when it is defined
  if (reset) begin
    sel_reg <= 1'b0;
    corr    <= 32'b0;
  end else begin
    sel_reg <= elt_sel;
    if (sel_reg) corr <= {15'b0, elt_reg} - blk_out[31:0];
  end
end

wire [31:0] acc_val = sel_reg ? {15'b0, elt_reg} : (blk_out[31:0] + corr);

Then leave the down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) textually unchanged except that every add_out_reg in them becomes acc_val, with the same bit indices.

Why it matches on every cycle. Write X[k] for a register's value in cycle k and g[k] for a combinational signal during cycle k; r is reset, E is elt_sel, A[k] = a_flopped[k]+b_flopped[k]. The original: s[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (E[k] ? A[k] : s[k]+m[k]) with m[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : a_flopped[k]*b_flopped[k]. The block, driven by a_flopped/b_flopped and by the same reset, has operand registers p[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (a_flopped[k], b_flopped[k]), so its product term p_a[k]*p_b[k] equals m[k] cycle for cycle (0*0 = 0 under reset), and out[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : out[k] + m[k] — the original recurrence with the eltwise branch removed. The invariant maintained is: sel_reg[k] implies s[k] = elt_reg[k], and otherwise s[k] = out[k] + corr[k] mod 2^32. It holds after any reset (all of sel_reg, corr, out, s are 0); it is re-established on an eltwise cycle (sel_reg[k+1]=1, elt_reg[k+1]=A[k]=s[k+1]); and on a non-eltwise cycle corr[k+1] is elt_reg[k]-out[k] = s[k]-out[k] when sel_reg[k] and is held otherwise, while both out and s take the same +m[k], so out[k+1]+corr[k+1] = s[k+1]. Only the low 32 bits of the block's modulo-2^37 sum are used, and the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of its addends, so the block's wider wrap is invisible. Latency, reset behaviour and out are therefore bit-identical on every cycle, including back-to-back eltwise cycles, eltwise->MAC transitions with no reset, and eltwise_mode & ~eltwise_add (where E=0 and the plain MAC path is used). No added register is relied on before the first reset: corr and sel_reg are reset-cleared, and elt_reg is read only when sel_reg is 1, which cannot happen before a reset-released eltwise cycle.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, replacing the 84 soft multiplies that synthesis had already put on 84 DSP tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode, one per tile. The mode holds two blocks per tile and all 12 blocks of a matmul slice see the identical reset expression (effective_rst | (eltwise_mode & ~ready_for_eltwise_op), conv_layer.v:2528/2001/2647) and the one clock, so they may legally pair into 6 tiles per slice (42 total); the baseline packer paired none of the equivalent pairs, so the count reported is the conservative 84 — the same tile count as the baseline, i.e. the rewrite is not expected to pay or save tile area, and its gain must come from delay and from the soft logic it moves.

Stages and the critical path. No two blocks are ever in series: the longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, 2.219 ns inside the block (input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup), well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The stages the rewrite leaves, per instance:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> block input register: 0.060 + 1.627 (register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> 17-bit elt_sum -> elt_reg: about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + 0.019 = 2.2 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> corr: 0.060 + 0.96 (block into soft logic) + 1.14 (one soft adder level) + 0.019 = 2.18 ns.
  • slowest: block out -> 32-bit add blk_out+corr -> sel_reg mux -> down-cast -> out flip-flop: 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + about two further soft levels (the 32-bit mux folds into the down-cast's per-bit LUT, whose inputs become sel/elt_reg bit/sign/overflow/sum bit — five, one LUT6; the 16-bit OR that forms the overflow flag is the other level) + 0.019, about 4.5 ns.

This is the one stage the rewrite lengthens: in the baseline the 32-bit add ended at add_out_reg and the down-cast was a separate short stage; here the add and the down-cast share a stage. It is deliberate — it is the price of putting the accumulator register inside the block — and at about 4.5 ns it still sits below the baseline's 5.57582 ns and below the 5.496 ns second-slowest stage. Both baseline stages that set those numbers are qmult multiply stages (register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop) in two different seq_mac instances; all 84 such stages are removed by this rewrite, since all 84 instances are converted, so the new worst stage is the one above.

Soft logic moved. Out of the design: mul_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), add_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), the two 32-bit add_in1/add_in2 muxes (about 64 LUTs) and the 32-bit qadd — these leave the baseline's slowest stage (the multiply stage, which disappears entirely) and the accumulate stage. Into the design: elt_reg (17 flip-flops), sel_reg (1), corr (32 flip-flops plus a 32-bit load mux), a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit adder. Net per instance is roughly -14 flip-flops, -94 LUTs and +49 carry-chain bits, so used logic-block area should land near the baseline's, with the gain coming from the roughly 20% shorter critical path; wirelength should move little, the block's 32 output bits now feeding two adders instead of one register.

Register stages, before and after. Before: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> product -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> sum -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4); the product is registered 1 stage before the sum. After: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> block operand registers (stage 2, where the product is formed) -> block accumulator out (stage 3) -> +corr/mux/down-cast -> out (stage 4). Identical count and identical latency; no register is added to or removed from the operand-to-result path, and the three added registers (elt_reg, sel_reg, corr) sit beside it, not in it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's eltwise-add result were written to its own register instead of overwriting the MAC accumulator (conv_layer.v:2807-2808), or if reset were guaranteed asserted at every eltwise-to-MAC boundary — which the conv_layer FSM in fact does, since start_mat_mul drops and clk_cnt returns to 0 while op still reads 2'b10, holding mac_reset high (conv_layer.v:1143-1155, 2001, 2647) — then the block's out could be used as add_out_reg directly, deleting corr, the 32-bit subtractor and the 32-bit output adder and taking about 1.1 ns off the stage above. The standalone seq_mac testbench does not honour that boundary, so the rewrite above cannot rely on it.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

APPROVE

Checked against conv_layer.v and sim_reference/:

  • Region/instances. seq_mac is instantiated once, in processing_element (2648); 12 live PEs per slice (2570-2588, three commented out per row) × 7 matmul_4x4_systolic (1309-1549) = 84. All converted, none left — stated.
  • Arithmetic/widths. DWIDTH=16 (14); qmult is an unsigned 16×16 → 32 (2849-2854), so {2'b0,a_flopped}/{3'b0,b_flopped} into the 18/19 unsigned ports is exact, and only the low 32 bits of the block's mod-2^37 sum are used, which is sound for addition.
  • Readers. a_flopped/b_flopped are read by add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808) and correctly kept outside; mul_out_reg is read only by add_in2; add_out_reg is read by the feedback mux and the down-cast (2823-2835), and the down-cast's reader is served by the recomputed acc_val.
  • The eltwise overwrite. Real (2807-2808) and not coverable by the block's no-load contract; the correction sel_reg/elt_reg/corr reproduces it. The invariant checks out on all four cases (reset, eltwise, non-eltwise after eltwise, non-eltwise after non-eltwise), including back-to-back eltwise and eltwise→MAC with no reset, which the directed stimulus does exercise in the verdict build (directed_conv_seq_mac.vh:13-15,20).
  • Reset/power-up. Block .reset = seq_mac's reset (the PE's mac_reset, 2647), matching every original register's synchronous clear; sel_reg/corr are reset-cleared and elt_reg is only selected when sel_reg is 1, so no added register's initial value is relied on. The testbench resets before cmp is enabled (tb:154-156).
  • No ASSUMES needed, and the plan correctly declines the FSM-based one: RESET_BETWEEN_PHASES is the diagnostic build only (tb:3,6; drive:7-10), and the FSM facts cited for the DESIGN_CHANGE note (1143-1155, 2001) read as described.
  • Required items all present: candidate disposition, 84 blocks / 84 tiles, longest block series 1 at 2.219 ns vs the ~4.5 ns slowest stage, the soft logic leaving the baseline's slowest (multiply) stage and the accumulate stage, and register stages before (input→a_flopped→mul_out_reg→add_out_reg) and after (input→a_flopped→block operand regs→block accumulator), identical latency.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Call 3 · editor · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 30 · out 6261 · reasoning 1061 · cache read 368458 · cache write 24253 · 85.0 s
prompt
Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int16). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 4.5
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (i.e. the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2777, zero-extended 16->18); .b({3'b0, b_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2778, zero-extended 16->19); .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] carries the running sum that the baseline held in add_out_reg. The block's operand registers replace mul_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781) and the block's accumulator replaces add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2806); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances and both of those registers are deleted.

PLAN:

Candidates. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (12 live PEs x 7 matmul slices; conv_layer.v:2570-2588, 1309-1585). All 84 are converted; none is left.

What the region is. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped register the operands; qmult forms the unsigned 16x16 product into 32 bits (conv_layer.v:2854 — both operands are plain unsigned vectors, so this is an unsigned product and matches the block's unsigned ports and zero-extension exactly, and 16x16 never exceeds 32 of the block's 37 bits); mul_out_reg registers it; qadd adds it to add_out_reg; add_out_reg registers the sum; a saturating down-cast and the out register follow. The accumulator recurrence add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is exactly what the block computes, except that add_in1/add_in2 (conv_layer.v:2807-2808) overwrite the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped whenever eltwise_mode & eltwise_add. The block has no load path and no enable, so that overwrite cannot go inside it, and the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add/reset independently (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:88-89, drive_conv_seq_mac.vh:5-11 — the verdict build forces no reset at a phase boundary), so no assumption about phase boundaries may be used. Hence T2: the accumulation goes into the block and the overwrite is reproduced outside it as a registered offset.

The rewrite (per seq_mac). Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their reset exactly as they are; they are read by the eltwise path, so they stay outside the block. Delete mult_u1, mul_out_reg, add_u1 and add_out_reg. Add:

wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a_flopped}), .b({3'b0, b_flopped}), .out(blk_out));

wire        elt_sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;
wire [16:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

reg  [16:0] elt_reg;    // the eltwise-add value, 17 bits is its full width
reg         sel_reg;    // 1 in the cycle whose accumulator value came from the eltwise path
reg  [31:0] corr;       // offset: accumulator value minus the block's running sum

always @(posedge clk) begin
  elt_reg <= elt_sum;                      // no reset: read only when sel_reg is 1, by when it is defined
  if (reset) begin
    sel_reg <= 1'b0;
    corr    <= 32'b0;
  end else begin
    sel_reg <= elt_sel;
    if (sel_reg) corr <= {15'b0, elt_reg} - blk_out[31:0];
  end
end

wire [31:0] acc_val = sel_reg ? {15'b0, elt_reg} : (blk_out[31:0] + corr);

Then leave the down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) textually unchanged except that every add_out_reg in them becomes acc_val, with the same bit indices.

Why it matches on every cycle. Write X[k] for a register's value in cycle k and g[k] for a combinational signal during cycle k; r is reset, E is elt_sel, A[k] = a_flopped[k]+b_flopped[k]. The original: s[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (E[k] ? A[k] : s[k]+m[k]) with m[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : a_flopped[k]*b_flopped[k]. The block, driven by a_flopped/b_flopped and by the same reset, has operand registers p[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (a_flopped[k], b_flopped[k]), so its product term p_a[k]*p_b[k] equals m[k] cycle for cycle (0*0 = 0 under reset), and out[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : out[k] + m[k] — the original recurrence with the eltwise branch removed. The invariant maintained is: sel_reg[k] implies s[k] = elt_reg[k], and otherwise s[k] = out[k] + corr[k] mod 2^32. It holds after any reset (all of sel_reg, corr, out, s are 0); it is re-established on an eltwise cycle (sel_reg[k+1]=1, elt_reg[k+1]=A[k]=s[k+1]); and on a non-eltwise cycle corr[k+1] is elt_reg[k]-out[k] = s[k]-out[k] when sel_reg[k] and is held otherwise, while both out and s take the same +m[k], so out[k+1]+corr[k+1] = s[k+1]. Only the low 32 bits of the block's modulo-2^37 sum are used, and the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of its addends, so the block's wider wrap is invisible. Latency, reset behaviour and out are therefore bit-identical on every cycle, including back-to-back eltwise cycles, eltwise->MAC transitions with no reset, and eltwise_mode & ~eltwise_add (where E=0 and the plain MAC path is used). No added register is relied on before the first reset: corr and sel_reg are reset-cleared, and elt_reg is read only when sel_reg is 1, which cannot happen before a reset-released eltwise cycle.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, replacing the 84 soft multiplies that synthesis had already put on 84 DSP tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode, one per tile. The mode holds two blocks per tile and all 12 blocks of a matmul slice see the identical reset expression (effective_rst | (eltwise_mode & ~ready_for_eltwise_op), conv_layer.v:2528/2001/2647) and the one clock, so they may legally pair into 6 tiles per slice (42 total); the baseline packer paired none of the equivalent pairs, so the count reported is the conservative 84 — the same tile count as the baseline, i.e. the rewrite is not expected to pay or save tile area, and its gain must come from delay and from the soft logic it moves.

Stages and the critical path. No two blocks are ever in series: the longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, 2.219 ns inside the block (input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup), well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The stages the rewrite leaves, per instance:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> block input register: 0.060 + 1.627 (register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> 17-bit elt_sum -> elt_reg: about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + 0.019 = 2.2 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> corr: 0.060 + 0.96 (block into soft logic) + 1.14 (one soft adder level) + 0.019 = 2.18 ns.
  • slowest: block out -> 32-bit add blk_out+corr -> sel_reg mux -> down-cast -> out flip-flop: 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + about two further soft levels (the 32-bit mux folds into the down-cast's per-bit LUT, whose inputs become sel/elt_reg bit/sign/overflow/sum bit — five, one LUT6; the 16-bit OR that forms the overflow flag is the other level) + 0.019, about 4.5 ns.

This is the one stage the rewrite lengthens: in the baseline the 32-bit add ended at add_out_reg and the down-cast was a separate short stage; here the add and the down-cast share a stage. It is deliberate — it is the price of putting the accumulator register inside the block — and at about 4.5 ns it still sits below the baseline's 5.57582 ns and below the 5.496 ns second-slowest stage. Both baseline stages that set those numbers are qmult multiply stages (register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop) in two different seq_mac instances; all 84 such stages are removed by this rewrite, since all 84 instances are converted, so the new worst stage is the one above.

Soft logic moved. Out of the design: mul_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), add_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), the two 32-bit add_in1/add_in2 muxes (about 64 LUTs) and the 32-bit qadd — these leave the baseline's slowest stage (the multiply stage, which disappears entirely) and the accumulate stage. Into the design: elt_reg (17 flip-flops), sel_reg (1), corr (32 flip-flops plus a 32-bit load mux), a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit adder. Net per instance is roughly -14 flip-flops, -94 LUTs and +49 carry-chain bits, so used logic-block area should land near the baseline's, with the gain coming from the roughly 20% shorter critical path; wirelength should move little, the block's 32 output bits now feeding two adders instead of one register.

Register stages, before and after. Before: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> product -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> sum -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4); the product is registered 1 stage before the sum. After: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> block operand registers (stage 2, where the product is formed) -> block accumulator out (stage 3) -> +corr/mux/down-cast -> out (stage 4). Identical count and identical latency; no register is added to or removed from the operand-to-result path, and the three added registers (elt_reg, sel_reg, corr) sit beside it, not in it.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"

// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the

// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.

module seq_mac(a, b, out, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add, reset, clk);
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;
input eltwise_mode;
input eltwise_add;
input reset;
input clk;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;

reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    a_flopped <= 0;
    b_flopped <= 0;
  end else begin
    a_flopped <= a;
    b_flopped <= b;
  end
end

//the multiply and the accumulate both live in the hard block now
wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a_flopped}), .b({3'b0, b_flopped}), .out(blk_out));

//the eltwise-add path overwrites the accumulator, which the block cannot do, so it is
//reproduced outside the block as a registered offset on the block's running sum
wire elt_sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;
wire [`DWIDTH:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_reg;         //the eltwise-add value, 17 bits is its full width
reg sel_reg;                     //1 in the cycle whose accumulator value came from the eltwise path
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] corr;        //offset: accumulator value minus the block's running sum

always @(posedge clk) begin
  elt_reg <= elt_sum;            //no reset: read only when sel_reg is 1, by when it is defined
  if (reset) begin
    sel_reg <= 1'b0;
    corr <= 0;
  end else begin
    sel_reg <= elt_sel;
    if (sel_reg) corr <= {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_reg} - blk_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];
  end
end

wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_val = sel_reg ? {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_reg}
                                           : (blk_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0] + corr);

wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;

//down cast the result
assign mac_out = 
    (acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
        (
           (|(acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
             {acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
             {acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-1] , acc_val[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
        )
        : //negative number
        (
           (|(acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
             {acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-1] , acc_val[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
             {acc_val[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
        );


always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    out <= 0;
  end else begin
    out <= mac_out;
  end
end

endmodule

Call 4 · acceptance reviewer · ACCEPT · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 10 · out 9448 · reasoning 7620 · cache read 88572 · cache write 25055 · 121.0 s
prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 84
STAGE_NS: 4.5
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (i.e. the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2777, zero-extended 16->18); .b({3'b0, b_flopped}) (conv_layer.v:2778, zero-extended 16->19); .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] carries the running sum that the baseline held in add_out_reg. The block's operand registers replace mul_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781) and the block's accumulator replaces add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2806); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances and both of those registers are deleted.

PLAN:

Candidates. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances (12 live PEs x 7 matmul slices; conv_layer.v:2570-2588, 1309-1585). All 84 are converted; none is left.

What the region is. In seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): a_flopped/b_flopped register the operands; qmult forms the unsigned 16x16 product into 32 bits (conv_layer.v:2854 — both operands are plain unsigned vectors, so this is an unsigned product and matches the block's unsigned ports and zero-extension exactly, and 16x16 never exceeds 32 of the block's 37 bits); mul_out_reg registers it; qadd adds it to add_out_reg; add_out_reg registers the sum; a saturating down-cast and the out register follow. The accumulator recurrence add_out_reg <= add_out_reg + mul_out_reg is exactly what the block computes, except that add_in1/add_in2 (conv_layer.v:2807-2808) overwrite the accumulator with a_flopped + b_flopped whenever eltwise_mode & eltwise_add. The block has no load path and no enable, so that overwrite cannot go inside it, and the reference testbench drives eltwise_mode/eltwise_add/reset independently (sim_reference/tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:88-89, drive_conv_seq_mac.vh:5-11 — the verdict build forces no reset at a phase boundary), so no assumption about phase boundaries may be used. Hence T2: the accumulation goes into the block and the overwrite is reproduced outside it as a registered offset.

The rewrite (per seq_mac). Keep a_flopped/b_flopped and their reset exactly as they are; they are read by the eltwise path, so they stay outside the block. Delete mult_u1, mul_out_reg, add_u1 and add_out_reg. Add:

wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a_flopped}), .b({3'b0, b_flopped}), .out(blk_out));

wire        elt_sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;
wire [16:0] elt_sum = {1'b0, a_flopped} + {1'b0, b_flopped};

reg  [16:0] elt_reg;    // the eltwise-add value, 17 bits is its full width
reg         sel_reg;    // 1 in the cycle whose accumulator value came from the eltwise path
reg  [31:0] corr;       // offset: accumulator value minus the block's running sum

always @(posedge clk) begin
  elt_reg <= elt_sum;                      // no reset: read only when sel_reg is 1, by when it is defined
  if (reset) begin
    sel_reg <= 1'b0;
    corr    <= 32'b0;
  end else begin
    sel_reg <= elt_sel;
    if (sel_reg) corr <= {15'b0, elt_reg} - blk_out[31:0];
  end
end

wire [31:0] acc_val = sel_reg ? {15'b0, elt_reg} : (blk_out[31:0] + corr);

Then leave the down-cast (conv_layer.v:2823-2835) and the out register (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) textually unchanged except that every add_out_reg in them becomes acc_val, with the same bit indices.

Why it matches on every cycle. Write X[k] for a register's value in cycle k and g[k] for a combinational signal during cycle k; r is reset, E is elt_sel, A[k] = a_flopped[k]+b_flopped[k]. The original: s[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (E[k] ? A[k] : s[k]+m[k]) with m[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : a_flopped[k]*b_flopped[k]. The block, driven by a_flopped/b_flopped and by the same reset, has operand registers p[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : (a_flopped[k], b_flopped[k]), so its product term p_a[k]*p_b[k] equals m[k] cycle for cycle (0*0 = 0 under reset), and out[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : out[k] + m[k] — the original recurrence with the eltwise branch removed. The invariant maintained is: sel_reg[k] implies s[k] = elt_reg[k], and otherwise s[k] = out[k] + corr[k] mod 2^32. It holds after any reset (all of sel_reg, corr, out, s are 0); it is re-established on an eltwise cycle (sel_reg[k+1]=1, elt_reg[k+1]=A[k]=s[k+1]); and on a non-eltwise cycle corr[k+1] is elt_reg[k]-out[k] = s[k]-out[k] when sel_reg[k] and is held otherwise, while both out and s take the same +m[k], so out[k+1]+corr[k+1] = s[k+1]. Only the low 32 bits of the block's modulo-2^37 sum are used, and the low 32 bits of a sum depend only on the low 32 bits of its addends, so the block's wider wrap is invisible. Latency, reset behaviour and out are therefore bit-identical on every cycle, including back-to-back eltwise cycles, eltwise->MAC transitions with no reset, and eltwise_mode & ~eltwise_add (where E=0 and the plain MAC path is used). No added register is relied on before the first reset: corr and sel_reg are reset-cleared, and elt_reg is read only when sel_reg is 1, which cannot happen before a reset-released eltwise cycle.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac, replacing the 84 soft multiplies that synthesis had already put on 84 DSP tiles in two_mult_18x19 mode, one per tile. The mode holds two blocks per tile and all 12 blocks of a matmul slice see the identical reset expression (effective_rst | (eltwise_mode & ~ready_for_eltwise_op), conv_layer.v:2528/2001/2647) and the one clock, so they may legally pair into 6 tiles per slice (42 total); the baseline packer paired none of the equivalent pairs, so the count reported is the conservative 84 — the same tile count as the baseline, i.e. the rewrite is not expected to pay or save tile area, and its gain must come from delay and from the soft logic it moves.

Stages and the critical path. No two blocks are ever in series: the longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, 2.219 ns inside the block (input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup), well under the baseline's 5.57582 ns. The stages the rewrite leaves, per instance:

  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> block input register: 0.060 + 1.627 (register to first block, median on this design) + 0.019 = 1.71 ns.
  • a_flopped/b_flopped -> 17-bit elt_sum -> elt_reg: about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + 0.019 = 2.2 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> corr: 0.060 + 0.96 (block into soft logic) + 1.14 (one soft adder level) + 0.019 = 2.18 ns.
  • slowest: block out -> 32-bit add blk_out+corr -> sel_reg mux -> down-cast -> out flip-flop: 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.14 + about two further soft levels (the 32-bit mux folds into the down-cast's per-bit LUT, whose inputs become sel/elt_reg bit/sign/overflow/sum bit — five, one LUT6; the 16-bit OR that forms the overflow flag is the other level) + 0.019, about 4.5 ns.

This is the one stage the rewrite lengthens: in the baseline the 32-bit add ended at add_out_reg and the down-cast was a separate short stage; here the add and the down-cast share a stage. It is deliberate — it is the price of putting the accumulator register inside the block — and at about 4.5 ns it still sits below the baseline's 5.57582 ns and below the 5.496 ns second-slowest stage. Both baseline stages that set those numbers are qmult multiply stages (register -> hard multiply -> one LUT -> flip-flop) in two different seq_mac instances; all 84 such stages are removed by this rewrite, since all 84 instances are converted, so the new worst stage is the one above.

Soft logic moved. Out of the design: mul_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), add_out_reg (32 flip-flops plus its reset gating), the two 32-bit add_in1/add_in2 muxes (about 64 LUTs) and the 32-bit qadd — these leave the baseline's slowest stage (the multiply stage, which disappears entirely) and the accumulate stage. Into the design: elt_reg (17 flip-flops), sel_reg (1), corr (32 flip-flops plus a 32-bit load mux), a 17-bit adder, a 32-bit subtractor and a 32-bit adder. Net per instance is roughly -14 flip-flops, -94 LUTs and +49 carry-chain bits, so used logic-block area should land near the baseline's, with the gain coming from the roughly 20% shorter critical path; wirelength should move little, the block's 32 output bits now feeding two adders instead of one register.

Register stages, before and after. Before: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1) -> product -> mul_out_reg (stage 2) -> sum -> add_out_reg (stage 3) -> down-cast -> out (stage 4); the product is registered 1 stage before the sum. After: module inputs -> a_flopped/b_flopped (stage 1, soft, unchanged) -> block operand registers (stage 2, where the product is formed) -> block accumulator out (stage 3) -> +corr/mux/down-cast -> out (stage 4). Identical count and identical latency; no register is added to or removed from the operand-to-result path, and the three added registers (elt_reg, sel_reg, corr) sit beside it, not in it.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if seq_mac's eltwise-add result were written to its own register instead of overwriting the MAC accumulator (conv_layer.v:2807-2808), or if reset were guaranteed asserted at every eltwise-to-MAC boundary — which the conv_layer FSM in fact does, since start_mat_mul drops and clk_cnt returns to 0 while op still reads 2'b10, holding mac_reset high (conv_layer.v:1143-1155, 2001, 2647) — then the block's out could be used as add_out_reg directly, deleting corr, the 32-bit subtractor and the 32-bit output adder and taking about 1.1 ns off the stage above. The standalone seq_mac testbench does not honour that boundary, so the rewrite above cannot rely on it.

response

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan and the rules:

  • Scope / plan fidelity — the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms nothing outside qadd, qmult, seq_mac changed, and the edited region (conv_layer.v:2774-2832) is the plan's text: mult_u1, mul_out_reg, add_u1, add_out_reg, add_in1/2, mul_out, add_out deleted; blk_out, elt_sel, elt_sum, elt_reg, sel_reg, corr, acc_val added; the down-cast and the out register are textually unchanged apart from add_out_reg→acc_val at identical bit indices. The unused qmult/qadd module definitions remain, which the rewrite does not need to touch.
  • Ports — all four inputs connected (conv_layer.v:2791-2792): .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2648, unmodified), .a({2'b0, a_flopped}) 18 bits, .b({3'b0, b_flopped}) 19 bits with DWIDTH=16; out drives blk_out, whose low 32 bits feed corr and acc_val as planned.
  • Visibility / terms — a_flopped/b_flopped stay outside the block and still feed the eltwise path; the eltwise overwrite (the term the block cannot load) is reproduced outside as elt_reg/sel_reg/corr. No deleted signal is read anywhere else in the file.
  • Same behavior — both operands are unsigned 16-bit vectors, so the original qmult product is unsigned and ≤ 2^32, matching the block's zero-extended unsigned ports; the block's operand register makes its term a_flopped[k-1]*b_flopped[k-1], exactly the original's mul_out_reg[k], so out[k+1] = r[k] ? 0 : out[k]+m[k]. The invariant sel_reg ⇒ acc_val = elt_reg, else acc_val = blk_out+corr, holds after each reset edge (all of out, corr, sel_reg cleared, original accumulator 0), is re-established on every eltwise cycle, and is preserved on non-eltwise cycles whether or not corr is reloaded — including back-to-back eltwise and eltwise→MAC with no reset. Only low 32 bits of the mod-2^37 sum are used, so the wider wrap is invisible.
  • Registers / reset / power-up — sel_reg and corr are reset-cleared; elt_reg is unreset but is read only when sel_reg is 1, which requires a prior non-reset edge with elt_sel high, by when it is loaded. No added register's initial value is relied on.
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed, and none is claimed; the rewrite does not depend on reset at a phase boundary (the reset_between_phases diagnostic also passes).

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
