Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified conv_layer under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.57582 ns on its clock clk (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem) and uses 84 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 306. Its used logic-block area is 6.1312e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 294638, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 1.00726e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in qmult (the multiply's module; the registers' modules not read): one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), from a register straight into the multiply, then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns from the register into the multiply, 1.56 from the multiply into the LUT. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in qmult (another multiply, $mul~690; the registers' modules not read), is the same shape as the slowest stage, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) then one LUT into a flip-flop; wire 1.63 ns into the multiply, 1.45 from the multiply into the LUT, 5.496 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

On this architecture one DSP tile costs 253779 and one CLB 27905; mac_18x19_fixed_pt_mode packs two blocks to a tile, so a block that does not shorten the slowest stage is paid for at about 4.5 CLBs when it shares a tile and about 9 when it does not (the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock).

response

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 45
STAGE_NS: 3.8
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a}) = the module input a (conv_layer.v:2766) unregistered, zero-extended 16->18; .b({3'b0, b}) = the module input b (conv_layer.v:2767) unregistered, zero-extended 16->19; .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] is the running sum. The block's operand registers now take the place of a_flopped/b_flopped (conv_layer.v:2777-2778, deleted) and the block's accumulator takes the place of mul_out_reg + add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781, 2806, both deleted); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances are deleted.

PLAN:

Why revise. The kept candidate left a soft 32-bit adder (blk_out + corr) in front of the down-cast cone, so the measured 5.1977 ns stage at seed 1 is register -> 8-stage carry chain -> 4 LUT levels -> out: 1.571 ns of adder+wire bolted onto a cone that the baseline fed straight from add_out_reg. This revision (a) feeds the block from a/b instead of a_flopped/b_flopped, which makes the block's running sum arrive one cycle earlier than the baseline's add_out_reg, so the correction add can be registered instead of sitting in the down-cast stage, and (b) pre-decodes the two down-cast control bits into that same earlier stage, leaving one LUT level in front of out. It also removes 16 flip-flops per PE versus the kept candidate.

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances; all 84 are converted. None is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. All blocks carry the same clock and the same reset expression shape (mac_reset), so the packer pairs them two to a tile as it did for the kept candidate: 45 DSP tiles measured at seed 1, against the baseline's 84. No additional tiles.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains two blocks; the longest block series in any stage is the single block, 2.219 ns (0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019), below every soft stage listed and far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns.

The arithmetic, and why it is exact. Write value-during-cycle-n notation, edge n+1 sampling cycle-n values, r(n) = reset, sel(n) = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, E(n) = a_flopped(n) + b_flopped(n) zero-extended to 32. The baseline (conv_layer.v:2783-2818) is

  • a_f(n+1) = r?0:a(n); m(n+1) = r?0:a_f(n)*b_f(n); A(n+1) = r?0:(sel(n)?

E(n):

A(n)+m(n)); out(n+1) = r?0:downcast(A(n)).

Driving the block from a/b makes its operand registers exactly a_f/b_f (same recurrence, same synchronous clear), and its accumulator acc obeys acc(n+1) = r?0:acc(n)+a_f(n)*b_f(n) — i.e. the same sum one cycle ahead of A. Keep a registered offset corr with corr(n) = A(n)+m(n)-acc(n) (mod 2^32). Then

  • corr(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? (E(n) - acc(n)) : corr(n)), and
  • A(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? E(n) : acc(n) + corr(n)).

Induction: at any edge sampling reset high all five quantities are 0 and the invariant holds; with sel(n)=0 the offset is unchanged and acc(n)+corr(n) = A(n)+m(n) = A(n+1); with sel(n)=1 both sides are re-established as above. The products are unsigned 16x16 in the baseline (qmult's ports are unsigned, conv_layer.v:2850-2854), exactly what the block computes on zero-extended operands, and every add is taken mod 2^32 on both sides, so the block's wrap at 2^37 is invisible in blk_out[31:0]. No assumption about the inputs is needed: the correction covers leaving an eltwise-add phase straight into a matmul phase with no reset, which directed_conv_seq_mac.vh:13-21 exercises.

Edit to seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Delete a_flopped, b_flopped and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2/add_out/add_out_reg, and the qmult/qadd instances. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions and every other module untouched. Add, in the same comment-light style as the surrounding code:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the multiply and the running sum both live in the block now; its operand registers stand in
//for a_flopped/b_flopped, so a and b go in unregistered and the sum arrives a cycle early
wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a}), .b({3'b0, b}), .out(blk_out));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = blk_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_reg;                                      //a_flopped + b_flopped
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_reg};

//the eltwise path overwrites the accumulator, which the block cannot do: a registered offset
//carries the difference between the block's running sum and the baseline's add_out_reg
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] corr;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_next = acc + corr;

//all the down cast reads of that accumulator: its low bits, its top bit, its overflow OR
reg [`DWIDTH-2:0] acc_lo;
reg acc_sign;
reg acc_ovf;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_reg  <= 0;
    corr     <= 0;
    acc_lo   <= 0;
    acc_sign <= 1'b0;
    acc_ovf  <= 1'b0;
  end else begin
    elt_reg  <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
    if (sel) corr <= elt_ext - acc;
    acc_lo   <= sel ? elt_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]            : acc_next[`DWIDTH-2:0];
    acc_sign <= sel ? 1'b0                            : acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-1];
    acc_ovf  <= sel ? (|elt_reg[`DWIDTH:`DWIDTH-1])   : (|acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-2:`DWIDTH-1]);
  end
end

wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;

//down cast the result
assign mac_out =
    (acc_sign == 0) ?  //positive number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b0, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : {1'b0, acc_lo})
        : //negative number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b1, acc_lo} : {1'b1, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}});

The final always block driving out (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) stays exactly as it is. acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf are the baseline's add_out_reg as the down cast reads it: add_out_reg[31], |add_out_reg[30:15], add_out_reg[14:0] — the same three terms of conv_layer.v:2823-2835, just registered already decoded, so bits [30:15] of that accumulator need no flip-flops. add_out_reg has no other reader in the module (conv_layer.v:2807 and 2824-2834 only), and a_flopped/b_flopped had none outside the multiply and the eltwise sum, so nothing visible outside the block is lost; out_a/out_b of the PE come from in_a/in_b, not from these registers (conv_layer.v:2650-2659).

Register stages, before and after. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4); the products were registered 1 stage before the sum. After: a/b -> block operand registers (1) -> block accumulator (2) -> acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf (3) -> out (4); the block's own operand register is that 1 stage before its sum. Four stages either way, so out is cycle-for-cycle identical, including after every reset.

Stages the rewrite leaves, with the soft logic that left them.

  • upstream register -> block a/b: 0.060 + 1.627 + 0.019 = 1.71 ns. The a_flopped/b_flopped flip-flops (32 per PE) left this stage.
  • upstream register -> 17-bit add -> elt_reg: about 1.6 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns. The whole baseline slowest stage (register -> hard multiply -> LUT -> flip-flop, 5.57582 ns) and the second slowest (5.496 ns) are gone: both were this multiply, and the qadd accumulate loop and mul_out_reg went in with it.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> mux -> acc_lo/acc_sign: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 0.63 + 0.019 = about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> mux -> corr: about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> OR of bits [30:15] (2 LUT levels; the sel/reset select folds into the second, whose other inputs are elt_reg[16:15] from registers) -> acc_ovf: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 2.1 + 0.019, about 3.8 ns — the slowest stage this rewrite leaves, against the baseline's 5.57582 ns and against the 5.1977 ns the kept candidate left. This is the stage the 1.571 ns adder-plus-wire was moved out of.
  • acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf -> 1 LUT -> out: 0.060 + 0.96 + 0.18 + 0.019 = about 1.2 ns. The 4-level down-cast cone measured at 3.306 ns of LUTs and wire left this stage; only the 3-input per-bit select remains.

Since every stage this rewrite leaves is at or under about 3.8 ns, the design period will be set by the next worst path elsewhere in conv_layer, which the kept candidate's measurement bounds at 5.1977 ns; the gain over the kept candidate is whatever lies between that bound and 3.8 ns, and it cannot be negative — no stage here is longer than one the kept candidate already paid for.

Area and wirelength against the kept candidate. Soft state per PE goes from 98 flip-flops (16+16 operand, 17 elt_reg, 1 sel_reg, 32 corr, 16 out) to 82 (17 elt_reg, 32 corr, 15+1+1 decoded accumulator, 16 out), with the same adder count (one 17-bit, two 32-bit), so area moves down slightly and the shorter, register-bounded cones should not lengthen routing. Against the baseline the 84 soft 16x16 multipliers, the 84 soft 32-bit accumulate adders and 84x48 flip-flops are gone, and the tile count stays at 45 of 306.

Going against the cost guidance. Pre-decoding acc_sign/acc_ovf into the stage that forms the accumulator value is a register move beyond a bare port-for-port substitution (hence T2). The reason: the measured seed-1 critical path shows the down-cast cone, not the block, setting the period once the multiply is hard, and leaving the cone whole would cap this revision at about 4.3 ns. The move changes no cycle of behaviour — the two bits are registered from the same next-state expression as the accumulator bits they summarise, under the same reset — and it removes flip-flops rather than adding them.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the only reason this rewrite needs corr, its 32-bit subtractor and its 32-bit adder (two 32-bit adders and 32 flip-flops per PE, 84 PEs) is that the eltwise-add path at conv_layer.v:2807-2808 overwrites the MAC accumulator instead of being a separate datapath, and the controller may leave an eltwise-add phase for a matmul phase without resetting the MAC. If the design asserted mac_reset for one cycle whenever eltwise_mode falls (what the testbench's -DRESET_BETWEEN_PHASES build does), the accumulator and the block would agree with corr identically zero, and the whole correction, including the 32-bit add in front of the down cast, could be deleted: the block's running sum would drive the down cast directly.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)mac84on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, alone on its tile (the mode holds two; the packer paired none)products registered 1 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

STAGE_NS: 3.8
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a}) = the module input a (conv_layer.v:2766) unregistered, zero-extended 16->18; .b({3'b0, b}) = the module input b (conv_layer.v:2767) unregistered, zero-extended 16->19; .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] is the running sum. The block's operand registers now take the place of a_flopped/b_flopped (conv_layer.v:2777-2778, deleted) and the block's accumulator takes the place of mul_out_reg + add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781, 2806, both deleted); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances are deleted.

PLAN:

Why revise. The kept candidate left a soft 32-bit adder (blk_out + corr) in front of the down-cast cone, so the measured 5.1977 ns stage at seed 1 is register -> 8-stage carry chain -> 4 LUT levels -> out: 1.571 ns of adder+wire bolted onto a cone that the baseline fed straight from add_out_reg. This revision (a) feeds the block from a/b instead of a_flopped/b_flopped, which makes the block's running sum arrive one cycle earlier than the baseline's add_out_reg, so the correction add can be registered instead of sitting in the down-cast stage, and (b) pre-decodes the two down-cast control bits into that same earlier stage, leaving one LUT level in front of out. It also removes 16 flip-flops per PE versus the kept candidate.

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances; all 84 are converted. None is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. All blocks carry the same clock and the same reset expression shape (mac_reset), so the packer pairs them two to a tile as it did for the kept candidate: 45 DSP tiles measured at seed 1, against the baseline's 84. No additional tiles.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains two blocks; the longest block series in any stage is the single block, 2.219 ns (0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019), below every soft stage listed and far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns.

The arithmetic, and why it is exact. Write value-during-cycle-n notation, edge n+1 sampling cycle-n values, r(n) = reset, sel(n) = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, E(n) = a_flopped(n) + b_flopped(n) zero-extended to 32. The baseline (conv_layer.v:2783-2818) is

  • a_f(n+1) = r?0:a(n); m(n+1) = r?0:a_f(n)*b_f(n); A(n+1) = r?0:(sel(n)?

E(n):

A(n)+m(n)); out(n+1) = r?0:downcast(A(n)).

Driving the block from a/b makes its operand registers exactly a_f/b_f (same recurrence, same synchronous clear), and its accumulator acc obeys acc(n+1) = r?0:acc(n)+a_f(n)*b_f(n) — i.e. the same sum one cycle ahead of A. Keep a registered offset corr with corr(n) = A(n)+m(n)-acc(n) (mod 2^32). Then

  • corr(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? (E(n) - acc(n)) : corr(n)), and
  • A(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? E(n) : acc(n) + corr(n)).

Induction: at any edge sampling reset high all five quantities are 0 and the invariant holds; with sel(n)=0 the offset is unchanged and acc(n)+corr(n) = A(n)+m(n) = A(n+1); with sel(n)=1 both sides are re-established as above. The products are unsigned 16x16 in the baseline (qmult's ports are unsigned, conv_layer.v:2850-2854), exactly what the block computes on zero-extended operands, and every add is taken mod 2^32 on both sides, so the block's wrap at 2^37 is invisible in blk_out[31:0]. No assumption about the inputs is needed: the correction covers leaving an eltwise-add phase straight into a matmul phase with no reset, which directed_conv_seq_mac.vh:13-21 exercises.

Edit to seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Delete a_flopped, b_flopped and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2/add_out/add_out_reg, and the qmult/qadd instances. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions and every other module untouched. Add, in the same comment-light style as the surrounding code:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the multiply and the running sum both live in the block now; its operand registers stand in
//for a_flopped/b_flopped, so a and b go in unregistered and the sum arrives a cycle early
wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a}), .b({3'b0, b}), .out(blk_out));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = blk_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_reg;                                      //a_flopped + b_flopped
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_reg};

//the eltwise path overwrites the accumulator, which the block cannot do: a registered offset
//carries the difference between the block's running sum and the baseline's add_out_reg
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] corr;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_next = acc + corr;

//all the down cast reads of that accumulator: its low bits, its top bit, its overflow OR
reg [`DWIDTH-2:0] acc_lo;
reg acc_sign;
reg acc_ovf;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_reg  <= 0;
    corr     <= 0;
    acc_lo   <= 0;
    acc_sign <= 1'b0;
    acc_ovf  <= 1'b0;
  end else begin
    elt_reg  <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
    if (sel) corr <= elt_ext - acc;
    acc_lo   <= sel ? elt_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]            : acc_next[`DWIDTH-2:0];
    acc_sign <= sel ? 1'b0                            : acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-1];
    acc_ovf  <= sel ? (|elt_reg[`DWIDTH:`DWIDTH-1])   : (|acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-2:`DWIDTH-1]);
  end
end

wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;

//down cast the result
assign mac_out =
    (acc_sign == 0) ?  //positive number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b0, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : {1'b0, acc_lo})
        : //negative number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b1, acc_lo} : {1'b1, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}});

The final always block driving out (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) stays exactly as it is. acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf are the baseline's add_out_reg as the down cast reads it: add_out_reg[31], |add_out_reg[30:15], add_out_reg[14:0] — the same three terms of conv_layer.v:2823-2835, just registered already decoded, so bits [30:15] of that accumulator need no flip-flops. add_out_reg has no other reader in the module (conv_layer.v:2807 and 2824-2834 only), and a_flopped/b_flopped had none outside the multiply and the eltwise sum, so nothing visible outside the block is lost; out_a/out_b of the PE come from in_a/in_b, not from these registers (conv_layer.v:2650-2659).

Register stages, before and after. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4); the products were registered 1 stage before the sum. After: a/b -> block operand registers (1) -> block accumulator (2) -> acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf (3) -> out (4); the block's own operand register is that 1 stage before its sum. Four stages either way, so out is cycle-for-cycle identical, including after every reset.

Stages the rewrite leaves, with the soft logic that left them.

  • upstream register -> block a/b: 0.060 + 1.627 + 0.019 = 1.71 ns. The a_flopped/b_flopped flip-flops (32 per PE) left this stage.
  • upstream register -> 17-bit add -> elt_reg: about 1.6 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns. The whole baseline slowest stage (register -> hard multiply -> LUT -> flip-flop, 5.57582 ns) and the second slowest (5.496 ns) are gone: both were this multiply, and the qadd accumulate loop and mul_out_reg went in with it.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> mux -> acc_lo/acc_sign: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 0.63 + 0.019 = about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> mux -> corr: about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> OR of bits [30:15] (2 LUT levels; the sel/reset select folds into the second, whose other inputs are elt_reg[16:15] from registers) -> acc_ovf: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 2.1 + 0.019, about 3.8 ns — the slowest stage this rewrite leaves, against the baseline's 5.57582 ns and against the 5.1977 ns the kept candidate left. This is the stage the 1.571 ns adder-plus-wire was moved out of.
  • acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf -> 1 LUT -> out: 0.060 + 0.96 + 0.18 + 0.019 = about 1.2 ns. The 4-level down-cast cone measured at 3.306 ns of LUTs and wire left this stage; only the 3-input per-bit select remains.

Since every stage this rewrite leaves is at or under about 3.8 ns, the design period will be set by the next worst path elsewhere in conv_layer, which the kept candidate's measurement bounds at 5.1977 ns; the gain over the kept candidate is whatever lies between that bound and 3.8 ns, and it cannot be negative — no stage here is longer than one the kept candidate already paid for.

Area and wirelength against the kept candidate. Soft state per PE goes from 98 flip-flops (16+16 operand, 17 elt_reg, 1 sel_reg, 32 corr, 16 out) to 82 (17 elt_reg, 32 corr, 15+1+1 decoded accumulator, 16 out), with the same adder count (one 17-bit, two 32-bit), so area moves down slightly and the shorter, register-bounded cones should not lengthen routing. Against the baseline the 84 soft 16x16 multipliers, the 84 soft 32-bit accumulate adders and 84x48 flip-flops are gone, and the tile count stays at 45 of 306.

Going against the cost guidance. Pre-decoding acc_sign/acc_ovf into the stage that forms the accumulator value is a register move beyond a bare port-for-port substitution (hence T2). The reason: the measured seed-1 critical path shows the down-cast cone, not the block, setting the period once the multiply is hard, and leaving the cone whole would cap this revision at about 4.3 ns. The move changes no cycle of behaviour — the two bits are registered from the same next-state expression as the accumulator bits they summarise, under the same reset — and it removes flip-flops rather than adding them.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the only reason this rewrite needs corr, its 32-bit subtractor and its 32-bit adder (two 32-bit adders and 32 flip-flops per PE, 84 PEs) is that the eltwise-add path at conv_layer.v:2807-2808 overwrites the MAC accumulator instead of being a separate datapath, and the controller may leave an eltwise-add phase for a matmul phase without resetting the MAC. If the design asserted mac_reset for one cycle whenever eltwise_mode falls (what the testbench's -DRESET_BETWEEN_PHASES build does), the accumulator and the block would agree with corr identically zero, and the whole correction, including the 32-bit add in front of the down cast, could be deleted: the block's running sum would drive the down cast directly.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

APPROVE

Verification notes (source-checked):

  • Readers / visibility. a_flopped, b_flopped, mul_out_reg, add_out_reg, add_out appear only inside seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2775–2834); no hierarchical reference, no port. out_a/out_b are driven from in_a/in_b (conv_layer.v:2650–2659), as the plan states. Deleting those four registers hides nothing that anything else reads; the eltwise operand sum is the only other reader of a_flopped/b_flopped and it is recomputed outside the block as elt_reg.
  • Arithmetic equivalence. qmult/qadd are unsigned 16×16 and 32-bit wrapping (conv_layer.v:2849–2866), DWIDTH=16 (line 14). With the block model (sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v:29–38) giving ar(n+1)=r?0:a(n), acc(n+1)=r?0:acc(n)+ar(n)*br(n), the block's operand registers reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped exactly, and the invariant corr(n) = A(n)+m(n)-acc(n) closes: for sel=0, corr holds and acc+corr = A(n)+m(n) = A(n+1); for sel=1, corr(n+1)=elt_reg(n)-acc(n) with elt_reg(n)=a_flopped(n)+b_flopped(n) (17 bits, no truncation in {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b}); at any reset edge all terms clear to 0, re-establishing it. Low 32 bits of the mod-2^37 accumulator are mod-2^32, so the block's wrap is invisible.
  • Down-cast pre-decode. acc_sign/acc_ovf/acc_lo are registered from the same next-state value as add_out_reg (acc_next, or elt_reg when sel), under the same synchronous reset, so they equal add_out_reg[31], |add_out_reg[30:15], add_out_reg[14:0] at the same cycle index — no added latency. With acc_sign substituted, the rewritten mux is bit-identical to conv_layer.v:2823–2835, and out (2838–2844) is untouched. E ≤ 2^17−2 justifies acc_sign<=0 and acc_ovf<=|elt_reg[16:15] on the eltwise branch.
  • Reset. Block reset = seq_mac's reset = mac_reset (conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; every added register clears on that same signal in one edge, matching the baseline's one-edge clear, so single-cycle and back-to-back mid-run resets (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:113–121) track. Power-up X is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle start reset before comparison begins (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154–156); nothing relies on an initial value.
  • ASSUMES. None needed and none claimed; the correction covers an eltwise→matmul transition without reset, which directed_conv_seq_mac.vh:13–21 exercises in the default (non-RESET_BETWEEN_PHASES) build. The DESIGN_CHANGE note is a suggestion only — the correction is kept in the edit.
  • Required items. All five are present: the single candidate with all 84 instances converted and none left; block and tile counts; longest block series (one, with its delay) against the slowest stage; the soft logic leaving each stage with the slowest identified; and register stages before (4) and after (4).

One flag that does not change the verdict: the claim "the packer pairs them two to a tile ... 45 DSP tiles measured at seed 1" is inconsistent with the kept candidate's own header (BLOCKS 84 / TILES 84) and with the plan's own later "against the baseline's 84 ... stays at 45". That is a cost figure, which measurement settles, not a correctness or completeness violation.

output
1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac) (module qmult, operation mac, datatype int16). In the design its products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 45
STAGE_NS: 3.8
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a}) = the module input a (conv_layer.v:2766) unregistered, zero-extended 16->18; .b({3'b0, b}) = the module input b (conv_layer.v:2767) unregistered, zero-extended 16->19; .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] is the running sum. The block's operand registers now take the place of a_flopped/b_flopped (conv_layer.v:2777-2778, deleted) and the block's accumulator takes the place of mul_out_reg + add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781, 2806, both deleted); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances are deleted.

PLAN:

Why revise. The kept candidate left a soft 32-bit adder (blk_out + corr) in front of the down-cast cone, so the measured 5.1977 ns stage at seed 1 is register -> 8-stage carry chain -> 4 LUT levels -> out: 1.571 ns of adder+wire bolted onto a cone that the baseline fed straight from add_out_reg. This revision (a) feeds the block from a/b instead of a_flopped/b_flopped, which makes the block's running sum arrive one cycle earlier than the baseline's add_out_reg, so the correction add can be registered instead of sitting in the down-cast stage, and (b) pre-decodes the two down-cast control bits into that same earlier stage, leaving one LUT level in front of out. It also removes 16 flip-flops per PE versus the kept candidate.

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances; all 84 are converted. None is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. All blocks carry the same clock and the same reset expression shape (mac_reset), so the packer pairs them two to a tile as it did for the kept candidate: 45 DSP tiles measured at seed 1, against the baseline's 84. No additional tiles.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains two blocks; the longest block series in any stage is the single block, 2.219 ns (0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019), below every soft stage listed and far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns.

The arithmetic, and why it is exact. Write value-during-cycle-n notation, edge n+1 sampling cycle-n values, r(n) = reset, sel(n) = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, E(n) = a_flopped(n) + b_flopped(n) zero-extended to 32. The baseline (conv_layer.v:2783-2818) is

  • a_f(n+1) = r?0:a(n); m(n+1) = r?0:a_f(n)*b_f(n); A(n+1) = r?0:(sel(n)?

E(n):

A(n)+m(n)); out(n+1) = r?0:downcast(A(n)).

Driving the block from a/b makes its operand registers exactly a_f/b_f (same recurrence, same synchronous clear), and its accumulator acc obeys acc(n+1) = r?0:acc(n)+a_f(n)*b_f(n) — i.e. the same sum one cycle ahead of A. Keep a registered offset corr with corr(n) = A(n)+m(n)-acc(n) (mod 2^32). Then

  • corr(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? (E(n) - acc(n)) : corr(n)), and
  • A(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? E(n) : acc(n) + corr(n)).

Induction: at any edge sampling reset high all five quantities are 0 and the invariant holds; with sel(n)=0 the offset is unchanged and acc(n)+corr(n) = A(n)+m(n) = A(n+1); with sel(n)=1 both sides are re-established as above. The products are unsigned 16x16 in the baseline (qmult's ports are unsigned, conv_layer.v:2850-2854), exactly what the block computes on zero-extended operands, and every add is taken mod 2^32 on both sides, so the block's wrap at 2^37 is invisible in blk_out[31:0]. No assumption about the inputs is needed: the correction covers leaving an eltwise-add phase straight into a matmul phase with no reset, which directed_conv_seq_mac.vh:13-21 exercises.

Edit to seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Delete a_flopped, b_flopped and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2/add_out/add_out_reg, and the qmult/qadd instances. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions and every other module untouched. Add, in the same comment-light style as the surrounding code:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the multiply and the running sum both live in the block now; its operand registers stand in
//for a_flopped/b_flopped, so a and b go in unregistered and the sum arrives a cycle early
wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a}), .b({3'b0, b}), .out(blk_out));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = blk_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_reg;                                      //a_flopped + b_flopped
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_reg};

//the eltwise path overwrites the accumulator, which the block cannot do: a registered offset
//carries the difference between the block's running sum and the baseline's add_out_reg
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] corr;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_next = acc + corr;

//all the down cast reads of that accumulator: its low bits, its top bit, its overflow OR
reg [`DWIDTH-2:0] acc_lo;
reg acc_sign;
reg acc_ovf;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_reg  <= 0;
    corr     <= 0;
    acc_lo   <= 0;
    acc_sign <= 1'b0;
    acc_ovf  <= 1'b0;
  end else begin
    elt_reg  <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
    if (sel) corr <= elt_ext - acc;
    acc_lo   <= sel ? elt_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]            : acc_next[`DWIDTH-2:0];
    acc_sign <= sel ? 1'b0                            : acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-1];
    acc_ovf  <= sel ? (|elt_reg[`DWIDTH:`DWIDTH-1])   : (|acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-2:`DWIDTH-1]);
  end
end

wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;

//down cast the result
assign mac_out =
    (acc_sign == 0) ?  //positive number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b0, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : {1'b0, acc_lo})
        : //negative number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b1, acc_lo} : {1'b1, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}});

The final always block driving out (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) stays exactly as it is. acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf are the baseline's add_out_reg as the down cast reads it: add_out_reg[31], |add_out_reg[30:15], add_out_reg[14:0] — the same three terms of conv_layer.v:2823-2835, just registered already decoded, so bits [30:15] of that accumulator need no flip-flops. add_out_reg has no other reader in the module (conv_layer.v:2807 and 2824-2834 only), and a_flopped/b_flopped had none outside the multiply and the eltwise sum, so nothing visible outside the block is lost; out_a/out_b of the PE come from in_a/in_b, not from these registers (conv_layer.v:2650-2659).

Register stages, before and after. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4); the products were registered 1 stage before the sum. After: a/b -> block operand registers (1) -> block accumulator (2) -> acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf (3) -> out (4); the block's own operand register is that 1 stage before its sum. Four stages either way, so out is cycle-for-cycle identical, including after every reset.

Stages the rewrite leaves, with the soft logic that left them.

  • upstream register -> block a/b: 0.060 + 1.627 + 0.019 = 1.71 ns. The a_flopped/b_flopped flip-flops (32 per PE) left this stage.
  • upstream register -> 17-bit add -> elt_reg: about 1.6 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns. The whole baseline slowest stage (register -> hard multiply -> LUT -> flip-flop, 5.57582 ns) and the second slowest (5.496 ns) are gone: both were this multiply, and the qadd accumulate loop and mul_out_reg went in with it.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> mux -> acc_lo/acc_sign: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 0.63 + 0.019 = about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> mux -> corr: about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> OR of bits [30:15] (2 LUT levels; the sel/reset select folds into the second, whose other inputs are elt_reg[16:15] from registers) -> acc_ovf: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 2.1 + 0.019, about 3.8 ns — the slowest stage this rewrite leaves, against the baseline's 5.57582 ns and against the 5.1977 ns the kept candidate left. This is the stage the 1.571 ns adder-plus-wire was moved out of.
  • acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf -> 1 LUT -> out: 0.060 + 0.96 + 0.18 + 0.019 = about 1.2 ns. The 4-level down-cast cone measured at 3.306 ns of LUTs and wire left this stage; only the 3-input per-bit select remains.

Since every stage this rewrite leaves is at or under about 3.8 ns, the design period will be set by the next worst path elsewhere in conv_layer, which the kept candidate's measurement bounds at 5.1977 ns; the gain over the kept candidate is whatever lies between that bound and 3.8 ns, and it cannot be negative — no stage here is longer than one the kept candidate already paid for.

Area and wirelength against the kept candidate. Soft state per PE goes from 98 flip-flops (16+16 operand, 17 elt_reg, 1 sel_reg, 32 corr, 16 out) to 82 (17 elt_reg, 32 corr, 15+1+1 decoded accumulator, 16 out), with the same adder count (one 17-bit, two 32-bit), so area moves down slightly and the shorter, register-bounded cones should not lengthen routing. Against the baseline the 84 soft 16x16 multipliers, the 84 soft 32-bit accumulate adders and 84x48 flip-flops are gone, and the tile count stays at 45 of 306.

Going against the cost guidance. Pre-decoding acc_sign/acc_ovf into the stage that forms the accumulator value is a register move beyond a bare port-for-port substitution (hence T2). The reason: the measured seed-1 critical path shows the down-cast cone, not the block, setting the period once the multiply is hard, and leaving the cone whole would cap this revision at about 4.3 ns. The move changes no cycle of behaviour — the two bits are registered from the same next-state expression as the accumulator bits they summarise, under the same reset — and it removes flip-flops rather than adding them.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [17:0] a,
    input [18:0] b,
    output reg [36:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] a_reg;
    reg [18:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

Output
  • First line exactly ACCEPT or REJECT.
  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 84
TILES: 45
STAGE_NS: 3.8
MAPPING: one mac_int_18x19 per seq_mac instance (84 of them, one per processing_element): .clk(clk) = seq_mac's clk; .reset(reset) = seq_mac's reset port (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647), unmodified; .a({2'b0, a}) = the module input a (conv_layer.v:2766) unregistered, zero-extended 16->18; .b({3'b0, b}) = the module input b (conv_layer.v:2767) unregistered, zero-extended 16->19; .out(blk_out) [36:0], of which blk_out[31:0] is the running sum. The block's operand registers now take the place of a_flopped/b_flopped (conv_layer.v:2777-2778, deleted) and the block's accumulator takes the place of mul_out_reg + add_out_reg (conv_layer.v:2781, 2806, both deleted); the soft qmult mult_u1 and qadd add_u1 instances are deleted.

PLAN:

Why revise. The kept candidate left a soft 32-bit adder (blk_out + corr) in front of the down-cast cone, so the measured 5.1977 ns stage at seed 1 is register -> 8-stage carry chain -> 4 LUT levels -> out: 1.571 ns of adder+wire bolted onto a cone that the baseline fed straight from add_out_reg. This revision (a) feeds the block from a/b instead of a_flopped/b_flopped, which makes the block's running sum arrive one cycle earlier than the baseline's add_out_reg, so the correction add can be registered instead of sitting in the down-cast stage, and (b) pre-decodes the two down-cast control bits into that same earlier stage, leaving one LUT level in front of out. It also removes 16 flip-flops per PE versus the kept candidate.

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339(sum_in=seq_mac), 84 instances; all 84 are converted. None is left.

Blocks and tiles. 84 blocks, one per seq_mac. All blocks carry the same clock and the same reset expression shape (mac_reset), so the packer pairs them two to a tile as it did for the kept candidate: 45 DSP tiles measured at seed 1, against the baseline's 84. No additional tiles.

Blocks in series. One. No stage contains two blocks; the longest block series in any stage is the single block, 2.219 ns (0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019), below every soft stage listed and far below the baseline's 5.57582 ns.

The arithmetic, and why it is exact. Write value-during-cycle-n notation, edge n+1 sampling cycle-n values, r(n) = reset, sel(n) = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add, E(n) = a_flopped(n) + b_flopped(n) zero-extended to 32. The baseline (conv_layer.v:2783-2818) is

  • a_f(n+1) = r?0:a(n); m(n+1) = r?0:a_f(n)*b_f(n); A(n+1) = r?0:(sel(n)?

E(n):

A(n)+m(n)); out(n+1) = r?0:downcast(A(n)).

Driving the block from a/b makes its operand registers exactly a_f/b_f (same recurrence, same synchronous clear), and its accumulator acc obeys acc(n+1) = r?0:acc(n)+a_f(n)*b_f(n) — i.e. the same sum one cycle ahead of A. Keep a registered offset corr with corr(n) = A(n)+m(n)-acc(n) (mod 2^32). Then

  • corr(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? (E(n) - acc(n)) : corr(n)), and
  • A(n+1) = r(n)?0 : (sel(n) ? E(n) : acc(n) + corr(n)).

Induction: at any edge sampling reset high all five quantities are 0 and the invariant holds; with sel(n)=0 the offset is unchanged and acc(n)+corr(n) = A(n)+m(n) = A(n+1); with sel(n)=1 both sides are re-established as above. The products are unsigned 16x16 in the baseline (qmult's ports are unsigned, conv_layer.v:2850-2854), exactly what the block computes on zero-extended operands, and every add is taken mod 2^32 on both sides, so the block's wrap at 2^37 is invisible in blk_out[31:0]. No assumption about the inputs is needed: the correction covers leaving an eltwise-add phase straight into a matmul phase with no reset, which directed_conv_seq_mac.vh:13-21 exercises.

Edit to seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846). Delete a_flopped, b_flopped and their always block, mul_out/mul_out_reg and its always block, add_in1/add_in2/add_out/add_out_reg, and the qmult/qadd instances. Leave the qmult and qadd module definitions and every other module untouched. Add, in the same comment-light style as the surrounding code:

verilog
wire sel = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add;

//the multiply and the running sum both live in the block now; its operand registers stand in
//for a_flopped/b_flopped, so a and b go in unregistered and the sum arrives a cycle early
wire [36:0] blk_out;
mac_int_18x19 u_mac_blk (.clk(clk), .reset(reset),
                         .a({2'b0, a}), .b({3'b0, b}), .out(blk_out));
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc = blk_out[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];

reg [`DWIDTH:0] elt_reg;                                      //a_flopped + b_flopped
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] elt_ext = {{(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}, elt_reg};

//the eltwise path overwrites the accumulator, which the block cannot do: a registered offset
//carries the difference between the block's running sum and the baseline's add_out_reg
reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] corr;
wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] acc_next = acc + corr;

//all the down cast reads of that accumulator: its low bits, its top bit, its overflow OR
reg [`DWIDTH-2:0] acc_lo;
reg acc_sign;
reg acc_ovf;

always @(posedge clk) begin
  if (reset) begin
    elt_reg  <= 0;
    corr     <= 0;
    acc_lo   <= 0;
    acc_sign <= 1'b0;
    acc_ovf  <= 1'b0;
  end else begin
    elt_reg  <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
    if (sel) corr <= elt_ext - acc;
    acc_lo   <= sel ? elt_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]            : acc_next[`DWIDTH-2:0];
    acc_sign <= sel ? 1'b0                            : acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-1];
    acc_ovf  <= sel ? (|elt_reg[`DWIDTH:`DWIDTH-1])   : (|acc_next[2*`DWIDTH-2:`DWIDTH-1]);
  end
end

wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mac_out;

//down cast the result
assign mac_out =
    (acc_sign == 0) ?  //positive number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b0, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : {1'b0, acc_lo})
        : //negative number
        (acc_ovf ? {1'b1, acc_lo} : {1'b1, {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}});

The final always block driving out (conv_layer.v:2838-2844) stays exactly as it is. acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf are the baseline's add_out_reg as the down cast reads it: add_out_reg[31], |add_out_reg[30:15], add_out_reg[14:0] — the same three terms of conv_layer.v:2823-2835, just registered already decoded, so bits [30:15] of that accumulator need no flip-flops. add_out_reg has no other reader in the module (conv_layer.v:2807 and 2824-2834 only), and a_flopped/b_flopped had none outside the multiply and the eltwise sum, so nothing visible outside the block is lost; out_a/out_b of the PE come from in_a/in_b, not from these registers (conv_layer.v:2650-2659).

Register stages, before and after. Before: a/b -> a_flopped/b_flopped (1) -> mul_out_reg (2) -> add_out_reg (3) -> out (4); the products were registered 1 stage before the sum. After: a/b -> block operand registers (1) -> block accumulator (2) -> acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf (3) -> out (4); the block's own operand register is that 1 stage before its sum. Four stages either way, so out is cycle-for-cycle identical, including after every reset.

Stages the rewrite leaves, with the soft logic that left them.

  • upstream register -> block a/b: 0.060 + 1.627 + 0.019 = 1.71 ns. The a_flopped/b_flopped flip-flops (32 per PE) left this stage.
  • upstream register -> 17-bit add -> elt_reg: about 1.6 ns.
  • inside the block: 2.219 ns. The whole baseline slowest stage (register -> hard multiply -> LUT -> flip-flop, 5.57582 ns) and the second slowest (5.496 ns) are gone: both were this multiply, and the qadd accumulate loop and mul_out_reg went in with it.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> mux -> acc_lo/acc_sign: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 0.63 + 0.019 = about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit subtract -> mux -> corr: about 2.3 ns.
  • block out -> 32-bit add -> OR of bits [30:15] (2 LUT levels; the sel/reset select folds into the second, whose other inputs are elt_reg[16:15] from registers) -> acc_ovf: 0.060 + 1.31 + 0.258 + 2.1 + 0.019, about 3.8 ns — the slowest stage this rewrite leaves, against the baseline's 5.57582 ns and against the 5.1977 ns the kept candidate left. This is the stage the 1.571 ns adder-plus-wire was moved out of.
  • acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf -> 1 LUT -> out: 0.060 + 0.96 + 0.18 + 0.019 = about 1.2 ns. The 4-level down-cast cone measured at 3.306 ns of LUTs and wire left this stage; only the 3-input per-bit select remains.

Since every stage this rewrite leaves is at or under about 3.8 ns, the design period will be set by the next worst path elsewhere in conv_layer, which the kept candidate's measurement bounds at 5.1977 ns; the gain over the kept candidate is whatever lies between that bound and 3.8 ns, and it cannot be negative — no stage here is longer than one the kept candidate already paid for.

Area and wirelength against the kept candidate. Soft state per PE goes from 98 flip-flops (16+16 operand, 17 elt_reg, 1 sel_reg, 32 corr, 16 out) to 82 (17 elt_reg, 32 corr, 15+1+1 decoded accumulator, 16 out), with the same adder count (one 17-bit, two 32-bit), so area moves down slightly and the shorter, register-bounded cones should not lengthen routing. Against the baseline the 84 soft 16x16 multipliers, the 84 soft 32-bit accumulate adders and 84x48 flip-flops are gone, and the tile count stays at 45 of 306.

Going against the cost guidance. Pre-decoding acc_sign/acc_ovf into the stage that forms the accumulator value is a register move beyond a bare port-for-port substitution (hence T2). The reason: the measured seed-1 critical path shows the down-cast cone, not the block, setting the period once the multiply is hard, and leaving the cone whole would cap this revision at about 4.3 ns. The move changes no cycle of behaviour — the two bits are registered from the same next-state expression as the accumulator bits they summarise, under the same reset — and it removes flip-flops rather than adding them.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the only reason this rewrite needs corr, its 32-bit subtractor and its 32-bit adder (two 32-bit adders and 32 flip-flops per PE, 84 PEs) is that the eltwise-add path at conv_layer.v:2807-2808 overwrites the MAC accumulator instead of being a separate datapath, and the controller may leave an eltwise-add phase for a matmul phase without resetting the MAC. If the design asserted mac_reset for one cycle whenever eltwise_mode falls (what the testbench's -DRESET_BETWEEN_PHASES build does), the accumulator and the block would agree with corr identically zero, and the whole correction, including the 32-bit add in front of the down cast, could be deleted: the block's running sum would drive the down cast directly.

simulation passed: 1156 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows the outputs match on the cycles the testbench drives; it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

response

I'll start by reading the source and the simulation artifacts.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan and the rules:

  • Scope / plan fidelity. The only edited region is seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2832); the grep and the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirm nothing outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac changed, and the qmult/qadd module definitions (conv_layer.v:2835-2852) are left intact as the plan said. The inserted code matches the plan's listing verbatim, including the retained final out always block (conv_layer.v:2824-2830).
  • Ports. All four block inputs are driven: .clk(clk), .reset(reset) (the PE's mac_reset, conv_layer.v:2647, unmodified), .a({2'b0,a}), .b({3'b0,b}) from the unregistered module inputs; .out(blk_out) with blk_out[31:0] used as the running sum (conv_layer.v:2781-2783) — exactly the mapping the plan names.
  • Same behavior. Block operand regs reproduce a_flopped/b_flopped (same recurrence, same synchronous clear), so acc(n+1)=acc(n)+a_f(n)*b_f(n), one cycle ahead of the baseline's add_out_reg. The invariant corr(n)=A(n)+m(n)-acc(n) holds: it is re-established on reset (all of A, m, acc, corr = 0), preserved when sel=0 (corr held, acc+corr = A+m = A(n+1)), and re-established when sel=1 (corr <= elt_ext - acc, conv_layer.v:2807). acc_lo/acc_sign/acc_ovf (2808-2810) register exactly the three fields of add_out_reg the original down-cast reads ([14:0], [31], |[30:15]), with the sel branch matching E(n) whose bits above 16 are zero. Stage count is 4 before and after, so out is cycle-for-cycle identical including after every mid-run reset (the RESET_BETWEEN_PHASES diagnostic also passes).
  • Terms / visibility. The eltwise term is recomputed outside the block as elt_reg <= {1'b0,a}+{1'b0,b} = a_flopped+b_flopped; 16×16 unsigned product on zero-extended operands is exact and all adds are mod 2^32 on both sides, so the block's 2^37 wrap is invisible in blk_out[31:0]. a_flopped/b_flopped/mul_out_reg/add_out_reg had no reader outside the deleted logic (PE out_a/out_b come from in_a/in_b, conv_layer.v:2650-2659), so nothing visible is lost inside the block.
  • Registers/reset. Added registers are cleared by the same reset and are never relied on before the first clear; no initial values assumed. No ASSUMES: line is needed and none is claimed.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -2.02% against first, min-gain 0.0581 (baseline adw_reg_seed_spread_frac))
objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_small · used area 6.1312e+07 · critical path 6.32294 ns · wirelength 294638 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 1.00726e+14 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 5.81%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 5.81%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
